But wait, let me reconsider once more. I want to make sure I&#x27;m applying the positive-inside rule correctly.

The positive-inside rule (von Heijne, 1986; Hartmann et al., 1989):
- For transmembrane proteins, positively charged residues (Lys, Arg) are preferentially found on the cytoplasmic (cytosolic) side of the membrane.
- This applies to the residues flanking the TM helix on the cytoplasmic side.

In a signal-anchor sequence (type II topology):
- The hydrophobic core spans the membrane
- If more positive charges are on the C-terminal side → C-terminal side faces cytoplasm
- If more positive charges are on the N-terminal side → N-terminal side faces cytoplasm

In our case:
- N-terminal side of TM (MNYIKAGK): 2 positive charges (K at pos 6, K at pos 8)
- C-terminal side of TM (KEEHDQKTKSKQK...): Many positive charges (at least 5-6 K&#x27;s in the first ~13 residues after TM)

Since the C-terminal side has more positive charges, it faces the cytoplasm.
Therefore: N-terminus is extracellular, C-terminus is cytoplasmic.
This gives: Short extracellular N-terminal, one TM, large cytoplasmic C-terminal.
= Option A.
